# Atomic Implementation Plan

## Priority 0 ΓÇö Establish contracts and prevent scope drift

1. Treat US and India as independent execution domains:
   - Separate refresh locks, cancel events, progress state, DB connections, worker pools, and derived-table writes.
   - Verify every refresh endpoint, frontend poll, and cancel request carries `market`.
   - Preserve `[]` as a deliberate no-op and `None` as full-market scope.
   - Ensure normal refresh never invokes full historical string, signal-matrix, or basket-history rebuilds.

2. Define canonical data contracts before implementation:
   - Current stock values: `stats`.
   - Historical stock values: `historical_screener`.
   - Current basket values: `string_screener_metrics`.
   - Historical basket values: `historical_string_screener`.
   - Position semantics: positive quantity/value for long, negative quantity/value for short.
   - Market prices must always come from real positive constituent prices; derived basket/index values must be clearly 
labeled as normalized values.

3. Create a computation truth table for:
   - Long-only basket return.
   - Long/short return.
   - Gross exposure.
   - Net exposure.
   - Dollar PnL.
   - Percentage return.
   - Fractional versus integer quantities.
   - Current versus historical values.

4. Explicitly exclude:
   - `intraday_backtest.py`.
   - Existing WTD Alpha calculation logic in `indicators.py`.
   - Any unrelated UI or strategy behavior not required by these issues.

---

## Priority 1 ΓÇö Refresh speed and market independence

### 1.1 Profile the current refresh pipeline

Measure separately for US and India:

- Universe synchronization.
- Daily-bar planning.
- Network download time.
- Bulk database writes.
- Vectorized stats.
- Fundamentals.
- Pre/post data.
- AI scoring.
- Aggregates.
- Historical stock screener.
- Historical string screener.
- Current string generation/metrics.
- Basket caches.

Capture:

- Symbol counts.
- Updated-symbol counts.
- Rows read and written.
- Database transaction counts.
- Worker counts.
- Peak memory.
- Cancellation latency.
- Per-stage progress timestamps.

Use the existing `get_db()` pragmas unchanged and record `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` for every changed large-table query.

### 1.2 Enforce a fast normal-refresh path

Normal refresh should:

1. Reuse a populated universe cache within the permitted age.
2. Plan stale symbols using indexed per-symbol latest-bar lookups.
3. Download only stale or missing symbols.
4. Group downloads by each symbolΓÇÖs own next required date.
5. Use the US IEX listed-equity path and exclude OTC.
6. Use pooled India sessions and background DB writing.
7. Pass the exact `updated_symbols` list to every downstream current and historical step.
8. Skip all downstream work when `updated_symbols == []`.
9. Recompute only:
   - Current stats for updated symbols.
   - Asset information for updated symbols.
   - Session snapshots for updated symbols.
   - Earnings or related symbol-scoped data.
   - Incremental historical rows for updated symbols.
10. Run aggregate updates only for affected data where possible.
11. Move expensive basket/string maintenance to explicit maintenance endpoints.

### 1.3 Separate historical maintenance from daily refresh

Create and document separate workloads:

- Daily refresh:
  - Current stats.
  - Incremental stock history.
  - Current string metrics only if required.
  - No full string-history rebuild.
  - No full signal matrix rebuild.
  - No complete basket-history rebuild.

- Explicit maintenance:
  - Full historical stock rebuild.
  - Signal probability matrix rebuild.
  - Historical string screener rebuild.
  - Basket close-pivot rebuild.
  - String universe regeneration.

The UI must clearly distinguish ΓÇ£refresh market dataΓÇ¥ from ΓÇ£rebuild historical strings.ΓÇ¥

### 1.4 Make historical string rebuild incremental

Audit `update_historical_string_screener` and its source cache pipeline.

Plan:

1. Identify whether each stringΓÇÖs latest historical date matches the latest available basket date.
2. Process only strings with missing dates.
3. Compute only the required warm-up window for incremental indicator calculations.
4. Insert only dates newer than each stringΓÇÖs existing maximum date.
5. Avoid rebuilding all 25,000 strings because one string or one schema version is stale.
6. Use chunked vectorized operations and bounded worker batches.
7. Avoid repeatedly loading the same close matrix.
8. Reuse memory-mapped pivot caches.
9. Add progress and cancellation checks at:
   - Cache construction.
   - Each string batch.
   - Each DB insert batch.
10. Version historical string logic independently from stock historical logic.
11. Permit a full rebuild only for:
   - Explicit user request.
   - Historical schema/meaning change.
   - Cache incompatibility.

### 1.5 Speed up 25,000-string generation

The current generation path should be redesigned around batch operations:

1. Load eligible symbols, prices, fractionability, and metadata once.
2. Remove per-symbol network calls and sleeps from generation.
3. Precompute circuit exclusions in one bounded market-level operation.
4. Generate all candidate constituent indices with NumPy.
5. Validate uniqueness and eligibility in vectorized batches.
6. Calculate quantities in vectorized form.
7. Build expressions and constituent rows in bulk.
8. Insert universe and constituents in large transactions.
9. Avoid deleting and recreating unaffected markets.
10. Keep deterministic seeds per market.
11. Add resumability:
    - Generation batch number.
    - Last generated string ID.
    - Market-specific status.
12. Separate:
    - Candidate generation.
    - Metrics computation.
    - Historical rebuild.
13. Ensure generation does not imply full historical computation.

---

## Priority 2 ΓÇö ETF and bond exclusion

### 2.1 Define a single exclusion policy

Create one reusable eligibility predicate for the entire website.

Exclude:

- Treasury securities.
- Treasury ETFs.
- Government-bond ETFs.
- Corporate-bond ETFs.
- Fixed-income ETFs.
- Bond mutual funds or equivalent assets.
- Any asset whose metadata identifies it as bond/fixed income.
- Known treasury/bond naming and classification fallbacks when metadata is incomplete.

The predicate must apply consistently to:

- Universe synchronization.
- `assets`.
- `stats`.
- Stock screener.
- String universe generation.
- Basket screener.
- Long/short screener.
- AI discovery.
- Portfolio candidate lists.
- Top lists.
- Any symbol search or detail navigation where applicable.

### 2.2 Decide whether exclusion is logical or physical

Preferred plan:

- Exclude at ingestion/universe eligibility so new derived data is never generated for invalid instruments.
- Add a migration/cleanup step to deactivate existing treasury/bond assets.
- Remove or mark stale derived rows from screeners and basket universes.
- Preserve historical data only if auditability is required, but hide excluded instruments from all website queries.

### 2.3 Add regression tests

Test:

- A known treasury ETF.
- A known bond ETF.
- A normal equity ETF, if equity ETFs are intended to remain.
- A normal stock.
- US and India separately.
- Current and historical modes.
- String and long/short generation.

---

## Priority 3 ΓÇö Long-only basket strings

### 3.1 Make standard basket strings long-only

For ordinary basket strings:

- Generate only positive constituent weights.
- Reject any existing negative constituent weight during regeneration or validation.
- Make the expression grammar explicitly long-only.
- Rebuild affected string universes.
- Ensure charts, metrics, constituents, and current details all use the same positive-weight definition.

### 3.2 Correct quantity and exposure accounting

For every basket position:

- `quantity` must be included in exposure.
- Long exposure = `qty ├ù entry_price`.
- Short exposure = `abs(qty) ├ù entry_price`.
- Gross exposure = sum of absolute position exposures.
- Net exposure = sum signed exposures.
- Do not use signed exposure when calculating gross capital usage.
- Ensure fractional positions are not silently rounded to zero.

Add API fields where necessary:

- Signed quantity.
- Absolute quantity.
- Position side.
- Entry value.
- Current value.
- Gross exposure.
- Net exposure.
- Dollar PnL.
- Return percentage.

---

## Priority 4 ΓÇö Correct long/short modeling and Alpaca quantities

### 4.1 Establish signed-position semantics

For LS strings:

- Long position: positive quantity/weight.
- Short position: negative quantity/weight.
- Positive underlying return:
  - Long contribution: gain.
  - Short contribution: loss.
- Negative underlying return:
  - Long contribution: loss.
  - Short contribution: gain.

The canonical contribution formula should be:

- Signed PnL = signed quantity ├ù `(current_price - entry_price)`.
- Or, for return series, signed position weight ├ù underlying return.

Validate both formulations against each other.

### 4.2 Fix fractional short handling

Because Alpaca does not support fractional shorts:

1. Preserve the target dollar allocation.
2. Convert short quantities to integer shares.
3. Use a deterministic rounding policy.
4. Ensure the resulting short exposure is recorded using the actual integer quantity.
5. Recompute residual cash/exposure after rounding.
6. Never display fractional short quantities as executable quantities.
7. Distinguish:
   - Analytical target quantity.
   - Broker-executable quantity.
   - Actual modeled quantity.
8. Test low-priced and high-priced symbols.
9. Test quantities that round to zero and define the minimum valid short position behavior.

### 4.3 Make LS PnL truthful

For every LS output:

- Compute total PnL from all legs, including shorts.
- Compute net return against the correct capital denominator.
- Decide and document whether the denominator is:
  - Initial net capital.
  - Gross exposure.
  - Allocated long-plus-short capital.
- Use the same denominator in:
  - Screener rows.
  - Detail pages.
  - Historical metrics.
  - Charts.
  - Leaderboards.
  - API responses.

The UI must not label weighted alpha as total return or use a proxy metric for true net return.

### 4.4 Prevent impossible negative chart prices

Do not chart signed portfolio value as a candlestick price.

For LS charts:

- Chart a positive equity curve or positive synthetic index.
- Start the series at a positive base, such as 100.
- Apply signed daily returns multiplicatively.
- Clamp or reject invalid non-positive values caused by bad data, while preserving the underlying PnL error for 
diagnosis.
- Keep ΓÇ£price/valueΓÇ¥ and ΓÇ£PnL/equityΓÇ¥ as separate concepts.
- Never allow OHLC `open/high/low/close` to be negative.
- Validate every generated OHLC row before API serialization.

---

## Priority 5 ΓÇö Correct basket chart price semantics

### 5.1 Separate actual prices from normalized basket values

The current basket chart uses a weighted basket/index value while the UI presents it like a stock price.

Choose one explicit presentation:

- If the chart is an index:
  - Label it ΓÇ£Basket ValueΓÇ¥ or ΓÇ£Normalized Basket Index.ΓÇ¥
  - Use a positive base value.
  - Do not call it the constituentΓÇÖs actual price.

- If actual constituent prices are required:
  - Display constituent prices in the contribution table and detail header.
  - Do not fabricate a single stock price for the basket.

### 5.2 Fix `$2`-style misleading displays

Audit:

- Currency prefixes.
- Normalization constants.
- `price` fields in basket metrics.
- Chart tooltip labels.
- String detail stat cards.
- Screener table labels.
- LS detail pages.

Use market-aware formatting:

- US: `$`.
- India: `Rs` or the projectΓÇÖs established India format.
- Basket: index/value label, not stock-price label.

### 5.3 Validate chart data

For each chart endpoint:

- Confirm chronological ordering.
- Confirm all OHLC values are positive.
- Confirm latest returned candle is the latest available date.
- Confirm the chart and metrics use the same source and normalization.
- Compare one basket against manually calculated weighted constituent values.

---

## Priority 6 ΓÇö Date filter for every screener

### 6.1 Inventory all screener types

Implement a shared date-filter contract for:

- Stock screener.
- Basket/string screener.
- Long/short screener.
- Any string strategy or derived screener that exposes historical rows.
- Leaderboard/date-based views where applicable.

### 6.2 Stock screener

Verify and preserve:

- `historical_screener` as the source for all historical indicators.
- `assets` only for metadata.
- `stats` only for explicitly current-only fields.
- SQL filtering before count, sort, and pagination.
- Historical sort mappings for:
  - `change_pct`.
  - `weighted_alpha`.
  - `next_day_return`.
  - `price`.
  - `volume`.

### 6.3 String and LS screeners

For date mode:

1. Resolve the selected trading date.
2. Query `historical_string_screener`.
3. Restrict LS rows to the LS universe.
4. Use historical LS values, not current metrics.
5. Apply all filters in SQL.
6. Apply sorting before pagination.
7. Return the same field shape as current mode.
8. Return `NULL` for fields without a historical source.
9. Return the selected historical date as `last_updated`.
10. Ensure `next_day_return` means the next available trading row.
11. Ensure long/short historical returns use signed position math.
12. Prevent current values from leaking into historical rows.

### 6.4 Date-filter UI behavior

Audit every template:

- Date picker submission.
- Date-option loading.
- Market propagation.
- Reset behavior.
- Pagination retaining `date_cutoff`.
- Sort links retaining `date_cutoff`.
- URL state restoration.
- Historical/current labels.

### 6.5 Required validation

For both markets and every screener:

- Select an older non-latest date.
- Compare one symbol/string over at least three dates.
- Verify indicators change as of the date.
- Verify next-day return against the next trading close.
- Verify ascending and descending sorting.
- Verify filters affect count before pagination.
- Verify latest historical row agrees with current bar data.

---

## Priority 7 ΓÇö Chart and layout redesign

### 7.1 Standardize chart sizing

Create shared chart layout rules for all non-intraday pages:

- Larger price-chart height.
- Larger Accel-chart height.
- Responsive sizing.
- Stable minimum height on desktop and mobile.
- Resize handling after layout changes.
- No page-specific small-height overrides.

Apply to:

- Stock details.
- String details.
- Basket details.
- Portfolio details.
- Any other chart-bearing pages.

### 7.2 Open charts at the latest candle

For every chart implementation:

- Load data chronologically.
- Set the visible logical range to the latest window after data load.
- Avoid `fitContent()` leaving the viewport at the oldest range.
- Define a consistent initial lookback.
- Preserve user zoom/pan after initial render.
- Ensure linked charts use the same visible range.

### 7.3 Link price and Accel charts

For stock, portfolio, and string detail pages:

- Use synchronized time scales.
- Synchronize crosshair movement.
- Synchronize visible logical ranges.
- Ensure matching date keys and timezone handling.
- Place price chart above Accel chart in the first fold.
- Keep the two charts visually connected with shared width and date axis behavior.

### 7.4 Add WTD Alpha chart

Add a third chart directly below Accel:

- Use the existing WTD Alpha data source.
- Do not change the WTD Alpha calculation.
- Display the series as an as-of-date historical value.
- Synchronize its time scale with price and Accel.
- Label units clearly.
- Add null/insufficient-data handling.
- Verify top-string values in millions are not caused by:
  - Volume being mistaken for WTD Alpha.
  - Weight scaling.
  - Unit conversion.
  - Missing normalization.
  - Integer overflow or dtype conversion.
- If millions are mathematically valid, label the units; otherwise correct only the consuming aggregation/display path.

### 7.5 Align stock and string detail layouts

Use the string detail layout as the structural reference:

- Header with identity and current price/change.
- Stat-card arrangement.
- Price chart.
- Accel chart.
- WTD Alpha chart.
- Signal/metric summaries.
- Constituent or related-stock tables.
- Historical/strategy sections below.

Do not copy basket-specific semantics into stock pages; copy layout and interaction behavior only.

### 7.6 Improve string detail header

At the top of string detail pages show:

- Current basket/index value or clearly labeled current value.
- Current change percentage.
- Market.
- String identifier.
- Expression.
- Long-only or long/short classification.
- True net return for LS strings where applicable.

## Priority 8 ΓÇö Computation audit

### 8.1 Audit source-to-output mappings

For every visible computation, trace:

- Input table.
- Input date scope.
- Formula.
- Weighting.
- Sign convention.
- Unit.
- Output table.
- API field.
- Template label.

Prioritize:

- Basket price/value.
- Daily change.
- Next-day return.
- Five-day return.
- Weighted alpha.
- ATR/ATRP.
- SuperTrend.
- Accel.
- Probabilities.
- Confluence.
- AI matrix.
- Long/short net return.
- Exposure and PnL.

### 8.2 Verify basket calculations independently

For selected strings:

1. Load ten constituent prices and weights.
2. Recalculate the basket manually.
3. Compare current value.
4. Compare daily return.
5. Compare five-day return.
6. Compare historical rows.
7. Compare chart OHLC.
8. Compare screener metrics.
9. Compare detail-page metrics.

### 8.3 Verify LS calculations independently

Use fixtures covering:

- Long-only rising basket.
- Long-only falling basket.
- Short-only rising basket.
- Short-only falling basket.
- Mixed long/short with equal exposure.
- Unequal exposure.
- Integer short rounding.
- Zero/near-zero residual exposure.

Expected directional behavior must be explicit:

- Long UP = positive PnL.
- Short UP = negative PnL.
- Long DOWN = negative PnL.
- Short DOWN = positive PnL.

### 8.4 Resolve WTD Alpha unit ambiguity

Do not modify the calculation itself.

Audit only:

- Where it is aggregated.
- Whether weights are normalized.
- Whether values are summed versus averaged.
- Whether the chart/table is reading the correct field.
- Whether millions are expected for a weighted volume-like metric.
- Whether labels incorrectly imply a per-stock scale.

Document the verified unit and update labels/API metadata if necessary.

   - Long/short metadata.

   - Exposure fields.
   - Historical string tables.
   - Versioning.
   - Market scoping.

2. Add only necessary indexes after query-plan measurements.

3. Add migration/version handling for:
   - Long-only standard strings.
   - LS signed-position semantics.
   - Historical string calculation version.
   - ETF/bond exclusion.
   - Chart source/normalization changes.

4. Rebuild only affected derived tables:
   - Standard string universe after removing shorts.
   - LS metrics/history after formula changes.
   - Historical string data after schema/meaning changes.
   - Never rebuild unrelated stock history during a normal refresh.

## Priority 10 ΓÇö Verification and rollout sequence

### Phase A: Safety and correctness

- India current screener.
- US historical screener.

- Older-date next-day return.

- No changes to WTD Alpha computation.
- No changes to `intraday_backtest`.

- A known treasury ETF.
- A known bond ETF.
- A normal equity ETF, if equity ETFs are intended to remain.
- A normal stock.
- US and India separately.
- Current and historical modes.
- String and long/short generation.

---

## Priority 3 ΓÇö Long-only basket strings

### 3.1 Make standard basket strings long-only

For ordinary basket strings:

- Generate only positive constituent weights.
- Reject any existing negative constituent weight during regeneration or validation.
- Make the expression grammar explicitly long-only.
- Rebuild affected string universes.
- Ensure charts, metrics, constituents, and current details all use the same positive-weight definition.

### 3.2 Correct quantity and exposure accounting

For every basket position:

- `quantity` must be included in exposure.
- Long exposure = `qty ├ù entry_price`.
- Short exposure = `abs(qty) ├ù entry_price`.
- Gross exposure = sum of absolute position exposures.
- Net exposure = sum signed exposures.
- Do not use signed exposure when calculating gross capital usage.
- Ensure fractional positions are not silently rounded to zero.

Add API fields where necessary:

- Signed quantity.
- Absolute quantity.
- Position side.
- Entry value.
- Current value.
- Gross exposure.
- Net exposure.
- Dollar PnL.
- Return percentage.

---

## Priority 4 ΓÇö Correct long/short modeling and Alpaca quantities

### 4.1 Establish signed-position semantics

For LS strings:

- Long position: positive quantity/weight.
- Short position: negative quantity/weight.
- Positive underlying return:
  - Long contribution: gain.
  - Short contribution: loss.
- Negative underlying return:
  - Long contribution: loss.
  - Short contribution: gain.

The canonical contribution formula should be:

- Signed PnL = signed quantity ├ù `(current_price - entry_price)`.
- Or, for return series, signed position weight ├ù underlying return.

Validate both formulations against each other.

### 4.2 Fix fractional short handling

Because Alpaca does not support fractional shorts:

1. Preserve the target dollar allocation.
2. Convert short quantities to integer shares.
3. Use a deterministic rounding policy.
4. Ensure the resulting short exposure is recorded using the actual integer quantity.
5. Recompute residual cash/exposure after rounding.
6. Never display fractional short quantities as executable quantities.
7. Distinguish:
   - Analytical target quantity.
   - Broker-executable quantity.
   - Actual modeled quantity.
8. Test low-priced and high-priced symbols.
9. Test quantities that round to zero and define the minimum valid short position behavior.

### 4.3 Make LS PnL truthful

For every LS output:

- Compute total PnL from all legs, including shorts.
- Compute net return against the correct capital denominator.
- Decide and document whether the denominator is:
  - Initial net capital.
  - Gross exposure.
  - Allocated long-plus-short capital.
- Use the same denominator in:
  - Screener rows.
  - Detail pages.
  - Historical metrics.
  - Charts.
  - Leaderboards.
  - API responses.

The UI must not label weighted alpha as total return or use a proxy metric for true net return.

### 4.4 Prevent impossible negative chart prices

Do not chart signed portfolio value as a candlestick price.

For LS charts:

- Chart a positive equity curve or positive synthetic index.
- Start the series at a positive base, such as 100.
- Apply signed daily returns multiplicatively.
- Clamp or reject invalid non-positive values caused by bad data, while preserving the underlying PnL error for diagnosis.
- Keep ΓÇ£price/valueΓÇ¥ and ΓÇ£PnL/equityΓÇ¥ as separate concepts.
- Never allow OHLC `open/high/low/close` to be negative.
- Validate every generated OHLC row before API serialization.

---

## Priority 5 ΓÇö Correct basket chart price semantics

### 5.1 Separate actual prices from normalized basket values

The current basket chart uses a weighted basket/index value while the UI presents it like a stock price.

Choose one explicit presentation:

- If the chart is an index:
  - Label it ΓÇ£Basket ValueΓÇ¥ or ΓÇ£Normalized Basket Index.ΓÇ¥
  - Use a positive base value.
  - Do not call it the constituentΓÇÖs actual price.

- If actual constituent prices are required:
  - Display constituent prices in the contribution table and detail header.
  - Do not fabricate a single stock price for the basket.

### 5.2 Fix `$2`-style misleading displays

Audit:

- Currency prefixes.
- Normalization constants.
- `price` fields in basket metrics.
- Chart tooltip labels.
- String detail stat cards.
- Screener table labels.
- LS detail pages.

Use market-aware formatting:

- US: `$`.
- India: `Rs` or the projectΓÇÖs established India format.
- Basket: index/value label, not stock-price label.

### 5.3 Validate chart data

For each chart endpoint:

- Confirm chronological ordering.
- Confirm all OHLC values are positive.
- Confirm latest returned candle is the latest available date.
- Confirm the chart and metrics use the same source and normalization.
- Compare one basket against manually calculated weighted constituent values.

---

## Priority 6 ΓÇö Date filter for every screener

### 6.1 Inventory all screener types

Implement a shared date-filter contract for:

- Stock screener.
- Basket/string screener.
- Long/short screener.
- Any string strategy or derived screener that exposes historical rows.
- Leaderboard/date-based views where applicable.

### 6.2 Stock screener

Verify and preserve:

- `historical_screener` as the source for all historical indicators.
- `assets` only for metadata.
- `stats` only for explicitly current-only fields.
- SQL filtering before count, sort, and pagination.
- Historical sort mappings for:
  - `change_pct`.
  - `weighted_alpha`.
  - `next_day_return`.
  - `price`.
  - `volume`.

### 6.3 String and LS screeners

For date mode:

1. Resolve the selected trading date.
2. Query `historical_string_screener`.
3. Restrict LS rows to the LS universe.
4. Use historical LS values, not current metrics.
5. Apply all filters in SQL.
6. Apply sorting before pagination.
7. Return the same field shape as current mode.
8. Return `NULL` for fields without a historical source.
9. Return the selected historical date as `last_updated`.
10. Ensure `next_day_return` means the next available trading row.
11. Ensure long/short historical returns use signed position math.
12. Prevent current values from leaking into historical rows.

### 6.4 Date-filter UI behavior

Audit every template:

- Date picker submission.
- Date-option loading.
- Market propagation.
- Reset behavior.
- Pagination retaining `date_cutoff`.
- Sort links retaining `date_cutoff`.
- URL state restoration.
- Historical/current labels.

### 6.5 Required validation

For both markets and every screener:

- Select an older non-latest date.
- Compare one symbol/string over at least three dates.
- Verify indicators change as of the date.
- Verify next-day return against the next trading close.
- Verify ascending and descending sorting.
- Verify filters affect count before pagination.
- Verify latest historical row agrees with current bar data.

---

## Priority 7 ΓÇö Chart and layout redesign

### 7.1 Standardize chart sizing

Create shared chart layout rules for all non-intraday pages:

- Larger price-chart height.
- Larger Accel-chart height.
- Responsive sizing.
- Stable minimum height on desktop and mobile.
- Resize handling after layout changes.
- No page-specific small-height overrides.

Apply to:

- Stock details.
- String details.
- Basket details.
- Portfolio details.
- Any other chart-bearing pages.

### 7.2 Open charts at the latest candle

For every chart implementation:

- Load data chronologically.
- Set the visible logical range to the latest window after data load.
- Avoid `fitContent()` leaving the viewport at the oldest range.
- Define a consistent initial lookback.
- Preserve user zoom/pan after initial render.
- Ensure linked charts use the same visible range.

### 7.3 Link price and Accel charts

For stock, portfolio, and string detail pages:

- Use synchronized time scales.
- Synchronize crosshair movement.
- Synchronize visible logical ranges.
- Ensure matching date keys and timezone handling.
- Place price chart above Accel chart in the first fold.
- Keep the two charts visually connected with shared width and date axis behavior.

### 7.4 Add WTD Alpha chart

Add a third chart directly below Accel:

- Use the existing WTD Alpha data source.
- Do not change the WTD Alpha calculation.
- Display the series as an as-of-date historical value.
- Synchronize its time scale with price and Accel.
- Label units clearly.
- Add null/insufficient-data handling.
- Verify top-string values in millions are not caused by:
  - Volume being mistaken for WTD Alpha.
  - Weight scaling.
  - Unit conversion.
  - Missing normalization.
  - Integer overflow or dtype conversion.
- If millions are mathematically valid, label the units; otherwise correct only the consuming aggregation/display path.

### 7.5 Align stock and string detail layouts

Use the string detail layout as the structural reference:

- Header with identity and current price/change.
- Stat-card arrangement.
- Price chart.
- Accel chart.
- WTD Alpha chart.
- Signal/metric summaries.
- Constituent or related-stock tables.
- Historical/strategy sections below.

Do not copy basket-specific semantics into stock pages; copy layout and interaction behavior only.

### 7.6 Improve string detail header

At the top of string detail pages show:

- Current basket/index value or clearly labeled current value.
- Current change percentage.
- Market.
- String identifier.
- Expression.
- Long-only or long/short classification.
- True net return for LS strings where applicable.

## Priority 8 ΓÇö Computation audit

### 8.1 Audit source-to-output mappings

For every visible computation, trace:

- Input table.
- Input date scope.
- Formula.
- Weighting.
- Sign convention.
- Unit.
- Output table.
- API field.
- Template label.

Prioritize:

- Basket price/value.
- Daily change.
- Next-day return.
- Five-day return.
- Weighted alpha.
- ATR/ATRP.
- SuperTrend.
- Accel.
- Probabilities.
- Confluence.
- AI matrix.
- Long/short net return.
- Exposure and PnL.

### 8.2 Verify basket calculations independently

For selected strings:

1. Load ten constituent prices and weights.
2. Recalculate the basket manually.
3. Compare current value.
4. Compare daily return.
5. Compare five-day return.
6. Compare historical rows.
7. Compare chart OHLC.
8. Compare screener metrics.
9. Compare detail-page metrics.

### 8.3 Verify LS calculations independently

Use fixtures covering:

- Long-only rising basket.
- Long-only falling basket.
- Short-only rising basket.
- Short-only falling basket.
- Mixed long/short with equal exposure.
- Unequal exposure.
- Integer short rounding.
- Zero/near-zero residual exposure.

Expected directional behavior must be explicit:

- Long UP = positive PnL.
- Short UP = negative PnL.
- Long DOWN = negative PnL.
- Short DOWN = positive PnL.

### 8.4 Resolve WTD Alpha unit ambiguity

Do not modify the calculation itself.

Audit only:

- Where it is aggregated.
- Whether weights are normalized.
- Whether values are summed versus averaged.
- Whether the chart/table is reading the correct field.
- Whether millions are expected for a weighted volume-like metric.
- Whether labels incorrectly imply a per-stock scale.

Document the verified unit and update labels/API metadata if necessary.

1. Review schemas and indexes in `db.py` for:
   - Long/short metadata.
   - Signed quantities.
   - Exposure fields.
   - Historical string tables.
   - Versioning.
   - Market scoping.

2. Add only necessary indexes after query-plan measurements.

3. Add migration/version handling for:
   - Long-only standard strings.
   - LS signed-position semantics.
   - Historical string calculation version.
   - ETF/bond exclusion.
   - Chart source/normalization changes.

4. Rebuild only affected derived tables:
   - Standard string universe after removing shorts.
   - LS metrics/history after formula changes.
   - Historical string data after schema/meaning changes.
   - Never rebuild unrelated stock history during a normal refresh.

## Priority 10 ΓÇö Verification and rollout sequence

### Phase A: Safety and correctness

- Market-independent refresh status/cancel tests.
- ETF/bond exclusion tests.
- Long-only universe tests.
- Signed LS PnL fixtures.
- Positive chart OHLC tests.
- Date-filter tests.

### Phase B: Performance

- No-change refresh timing.
- One-market refresh while the other remains idle.
- Incremental refresh with zero, one, and many updated symbols.
- 25,000-string generation benchmark.
- Historical string incremental rebuild benchmark.
- Full maintenance rebuild benchmark.
- Query plans for current and historical screeners.

### Phase C: UI

- Latest-candle viewport.
- Chart heights.
- Linked price/Accel/WTD Alpha charts.
- Stock/string layout parity.
- Current price/change visibility.
- US/India formatting.
- LS labels and true-return display.

### Phase D: Regression checklist

- `python -m py_compile` on all changed Python files.
- US current screener.
- India current screener.
- US historical screener.
- India historical screener.
- Basket historical screener.
- Long/short historical screener.
- Ascending/descending sorts.
- Filters before pagination.
- Refresh status independence.
- Market-specific cancellation.
- Latest historical row versus current bar.
- Older-date next-day return.
- No treasury/bond instruments anywhere.
- No changes to WTD Alpha computation.
- No changes to `intraday_backtest`.
